│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[solver]'.                                       │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │

│     • tclsh is not present on the system (no tclsh found). The autosetup     │
│       tree contains jimsh sources (jimsh0.c), so the build may either need a │
│       system tclsh or build & use the in-tree jimsh.                         │
│  • Dev headers / libraries:                                                  │
│     • readline headers not present under /usr/include/readline (so building  │
│       the shell with readline may require installing dev headers or          │
│       adjusting build flags).                                                │
│     • zlib header exists: /usr/include/zlib.h (zlib likely available).       │
│  • Filesystem, permissions, PATH:                                            │
│     • /app/sqlite does not yet exist.                                        │
│     • /usr/local/bin and /opt/venv/bin are writable. PATH contains           │
│       /opt/venv/bin before /usr/local/bin, so installing the sqlite3 binary  │
│       to either of those will make it available in PATH.                     │
│     • Plenty of disk space on / (overlay) and /app.                          │
│  • Build implications for coverage instrumentation:                          │
│     • gcov is available and GCC supports coverage; to instrument builds for  │
│       gcov one must pass appropriate coverage flags (e.g., -fprofile-arcs    │
│       -ftest-coverage or -coverage flags) via CFLAGS and ensure linking      │
│       includes coverage support. main.mk/Makefile.linux-generic accept       │
│       CFLAGS and CFLAGS.core and other per-feature flags, so coverage flags  │
│       can be injected through make variables.                                │
│                                                                              │
│ Commands I ran (summary)                                                     │

│  • Inject gcov coverage flags via appropriate make variables (CFLAGS /       │
│    CFLAGS.core or similar) when building so gcov instrumentation is enabled. │
│    Linker flags may also be needed.                                          │
│  • Install resulting sqlite3 into a writable PATH directory (e.g.,           │
│    /opt/venv/bin or /usr/local/bin). PATH precedence suggests /opt/venv/bin  │
│    is first.                                                                 │
│  • If readline support is desired for the sqlite shell, the readline         │
│    development headers are not present and would need to be provided or      │
│    disabled in the build flags.                                              │

│ ┃    Rather that stuffing all CFLAGS and LDFLAGS into a single set, we     ┃ │

│ ┃   break them down on a per-feature basis and expect the build targets    ┃ │

│ ┃                       to use the one(s) it needs.                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ -Wl,$(prefix)/lib LDFLAGS.pthread ?= -lpthread LDFLAGS.dlopen ?= -ldl        │
│ LDFLAGS.shlib ?= -shared LDFLAGS.rt ?= # nanosleep on some platforms         │
│ LDFLAGS.icu ?= # -licui18n -licuuc -licudata CFLAGS.icu ?=                   │
│ LDFLAGS.libsqlite3.soname ?= # see                                           │

│ ┃   during installation, which may break the build of targets which are    ┃ │

│  • Extract the provided SQLite source snapshot into /app/sqlite.             │
│  • Build the sqlite3 CLI with gcov coverage instrumentation (GCC's           │
│    -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage and -g).                                   │
│  • Resolve build bootstrap needs (no system tclsh: use in-tree               │
│    autosetup/jimsh).                                                         │
│  • Handle missing readline dev library by disabling readline in the shell    │
│    link step.                                                                │
│  • Copy the produced sqlite3 binary to a directory already in PATH           │
│    (/opt/venv/bin) and verify it runs.                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ What I ran (key commands and reasoning)                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Extract the vendored tarball into /app/sqlite and confirm VERSION:        │
│     • mkdir -p /app/sqlite                                                   │
│     • tar -xzf /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz -C /app/sqlite       │
│       --strip-components=1                                                   │
│     • cat /app/sqlite/VERSION Reason: use the pre-vendored snapshot (no      │
│       network).                                                              │
│  2 Inspected the provided Makefile templates (main.mk and                    │
│    Makefile.linux-generic) and autosetup to see how to inject coverage flags │
│    and how code generators are invoked (they use a tclsh / jimsh). Found     │
│    there is autosetup/jimsh0.c to bootstrap code generation if no system     │
│    tclsh exists.                                                             │
│  3 Built sqlite3 with gcov flags, while avoiding linking to readline (system │
│    headers/libs not present). Command used:                                  │
│     • make -f Makefile.linux-generic sqlite3                                 │
│       CFLAGS="-g -O0 -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage"                         │
│       CFLAGS.core="-fPIC -g -O0 -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage"              │
│       LDFLAGS="-fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage"                               │
│       LDFLAGS.readline= CFLAGS.readline= SHELL_OPT="-DSQLITE_HAVE_ZLIB=1"    │
│       -j$(nproc) Notes:                                                      │
│     • The build automatically compiled a bootstrap ./jimsh from              │
│       autosetup/jimsh0.c (used for the tcl-based code generation steps).     │
│     • I disabled linking to -lreadline (LDFLAGS.readline= and                │
│       CFLAGS.readline=) because the environment lacks the readline dev       │
│       library and the initial link failed with "cannot find -lreadline".     │
│     • Coverage flags were applied to both compile and link lines so .gcno    │
│       files are emitted and linking includes coverage support.               │
│  4 Installed the built binary into a directory in PATH:                      │
│     • cp /app/sqlite/sqlite3 /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3                           │
│     • chmod 755 /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 Reason: /opt/venv/bin is writable and  │
│       earlier in PATH, so the installed binary will be picked up.            │
│                                                                              │
│ What succeeded / verification                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • The source was extracted and VERSION is 3.50.4.                           │
│  • The build completed (after disabling readline), producing                 │
│    /app/sqlite/sqlite3.                                                      │
│  • Coverage instrumentation artifacts (.gcno) were generated during          │
│    compilation:                                                              │
│     • ./sqlite3-shell.gcno                                                   │
│     • ./sqlite3-sqlite3.gcno                                                 │
│  • Installed binary is available in PATH and reports its version:            │
│     • /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 -> version printed as "3.50.4 ..." when run.     │
│  • Running which sqlite3 returned /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 and sqlite3          │
│    --version succeeded.                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│ Where things are                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Source tree: /app/sqlite                                                  │
│  • Built sqlite3 (instrumented): /app/sqlite/sqlite3                         │
│  • Installed, available in PATH: /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3                       │
│  • Coverage metadata files (.gcno) present alongside build artifacts in      │
│    /app/sqlite                                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ Notes and possible follow-ups                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • If you want the sqlite shell linked with readline support (history, line  │
│    editing), install the system readline development package in the          │
│    environment (so -lreadline is available) and rebuild without clearing     │
│    LDFLAGS.readline / CFLAGS.readline.                                       │
│  • To produce .gcda coverage data you must run the instrumented binary       │
│    (e.g., run tests or exercise code paths). After running the instrumented  │
│    program, gcov / lcov / genhtml can process the generated .gcda files to   │
│    produce coverage reports. Example (outside this run):                     │
│     • Run some SQLite operations that exercise the code paths:               │
│       /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 some-db 'SQL;'                                   │
│     • Then run gcov in the source dir to get coverage summaries (gcov will   │
│       consume the .gcno and .gcda files).                                    │
│  • The build used the in-tree jimsh bootstrap (no system tclsh required).    │
│                                                                              │
│ If you want, I can:                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Rebuild enabling readline (after installing readline dev),                │
│  • Run a small workload to produce .gcda files and show an example gcov      │
│    report,                                                                   │
│  • Install into /usr/local/bin instead of /opt/venv/bin.                     │
│                                                                              │
│ Summary of performed work                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extracted /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz into /app/sqlite.      │
│  • Built sqlite3 with gcov instrumentation (-fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage,  │
│    -g) and handled autosetup/jimsh bootstrap.                                │
│  • Disabled readline linking due to missing system readline dev library to   │
│    allow successful build.                                                   │
│  • Installed the instrumented sqlite3 into /opt/venv/bin which is in PATH    │
│    and verified it runs and reports version. [tTester]                       │

│ instrumentation and make the resulting sqlite3 CLI available in              │

│ ensure code-generation/bootstrap works without a system tclsh (use in-tree   │
│ jimsh).\n- Disable readline linking (no readline dev headers present) to     │
│ allow a successful build.\n- Install the instrumented binary into a writable │
│ PATH directory (/opt/venv/bin).\n- Verify build artifacts and that running   │
│ the binary produces .gcda files.\n\nExecution (high-level)\n- Extracted      │
│ /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz into /app/sqlite (VERSION =         │
│ 3.50.4).\n- Built using the provided Makefile templates, injecting coverage  │
│ flags:\n  - CFLAGS="-g -O0 -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage"\n  - CFLAGS.core  │
│ also set similarly; LDFLAGS included coverage flags.\n- The build            │
│ bootstrapped code generation using the in-tree jimsh (no system tclsh        │
│ required).\n- Readline linking was disabled (LDFLAGS.readline=               │
│ CFLAGS.readline=) because readline development headers/libs were absent.\n-  │
│ Copied the resulting sqlite3 to /opt/venv/bin (writable and earlier in       │
│ PATH).\n\nResults and verification\n- Source tree: /app/sqlite (VERSION      │

│ sqlite3-shell.gcno, sqlite3-sqlite3.gcno).\n- Running the instrumented       │
│ binary produced runtime coverage data (.gcda files), e.g.,                   │
│ sqlite3-shell.gcda and sqlite3-sqlite3.gcda.\n- Basic functionality verified │
│ by creating/opening a DB and executing SQL via the installed sqlite3.\n\nKey │
│ findings / lessons\n- The provided tarball was usable directly; no network   │
│ fetch needed.\n- System tclsh was not present, but the in-tree jimsh         │
│ bootstrap allowed code generation to proceed.\n- Readline development        │
│ headers were missing; the build was simplified by disabling readline         │
│ linking. To get readline support, install the system readline dev package    │
│ and rebuild.\n- Coverage requires instrumentation flags both at compile and  │

│ Summary of work, execution, and test results                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Goal                                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  • Build SQLite from the provided vendor snapshot with gcov instrumentation  │
│    and make the resulting sqlite3 CLI available in PATH.                     │
│                                                                              │
│ Plan                                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extract the pre-vendored source into /app/sqlite.                         │
│  • Build sqlite3 with GCC coverage flags (-fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage     │
│    plus -g) and ensure code-generation/bootstrap works without a system      │
│    tclsh (use in-tree jimsh).                                                │
│  • Disable readline linking (no readline dev headers present) to allow a     │
│    successful build.                                                         │
│  • Install the instrumented binary into a writable PATH directory            │
│    (/opt/venv/bin).                                                          │
│  • Verify build artifacts and that running the binary produces .gcda files.  │
│                                                                              │
│ Execution (high-level)                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extracted /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz into /app/sqlite       │
│    (VERSION = 3.50.4).                                                       │
│  • Built using the provided Makefile templates, injecting coverage flags:    │
│     • CFLAGS="-g -O0 -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage"                         │
│     • CFLAGS.core also set similarly; LDFLAGS included coverage flags.       │
│  • The build bootstrapped code generation using the in-tree jimsh (no system │
│    tclsh required).                                                          │
│  • Readline linking was disabled (LDFLAGS.readline= CFLAGS.readline=)        │
│    because readline development headers/libs were absent.                    │
│  • Copied the resulting sqlite3 to /opt/venv/bin (writable and earlier in    │
│    PATH).                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Results and verification                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • Source tree: /app/sqlite (VERSION 3.50.4).                                │
│  • Built instrumented binary: /app/sqlite/sqlite3.                           │
│  • Installed and available in PATH: /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3 (which sqlite3 ->  │
│    /opt/venv/bin/sqlite3; sqlite3 --version -> 3.50.4).                      │
│  • Coverage build artifacts generated at compile time: .gcno files in        │
│    /app/sqlite (e.g., sqlite3-shell.gcno, sqlite3-sqlite3.gcno).             │
│  • Running the instrumented binary produced runtime coverage data (.gcda     │
│    files), e.g., sqlite3-shell.gcda and sqlite3-sqlite3.gcda.                │
│  • Basic functionality verified by creating/opening a DB and executing SQL   │
│    via the installed sqlite3.                                                │
│                                                                              │
│ Key findings / lessons                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • The provided tarball was usable directly; no network fetch needed.        │
│  • System tclsh was not present, but the in-tree jimsh bootstrap allowed     │
│    code generation to proceed.                                               │
│  • Readline development headers were missing; the build was simplified by    │
│    disabling readline linking. To get readline support, install the system   │
│    readline dev package and rebuild.                                         │
│  • Coverage requires instrumentation flags both at compile and link time;    │
│    .gcno appear at build time, .gcda appear only after exercising the        │
│    binary.                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ Possible follow-ups (optional)                                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Rebuild with readline support after installing readline dev headers.      │
│  • Run a larger test workload and generate human-readable coverage reports   │
│    with gcov/lcov/genhtml.                                                   │
│  • Install the binary to /usr/local/bin instead of /opt/venv/bin if          │
│    preferred.                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│ Status                                                                       │
│                                                                              │
